&\qquad+\underbrace{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[\left(\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}})}}\right)^{3}-\left(\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}}\right)^{3}\right]\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}{\bm{\phi}}_{t}^{\top}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}}_{{\bm{H}}_{2,T}}.\end{split}

(B.11)

where
{\mathbb{E}}_{t}[\cdot]={\mathbb{E}}[\cdot|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]
for simplicity.

Since
\nu_{t},{\bm{\theta}}_{t-1}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}
, from Algorithm
1
, we have
\sigma_{t},w_{t},\tau_{t}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}
.

Analysis of
{\bm{H}}_{1,T}

Notice that for any unit norm
{\bm{v}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, it follows that

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{1,T}{\bm{v}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[1-\left(\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}}\right)^{3}\right]\left\langle\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}},{\bm{v}}\right\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 3\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[1-\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}}\right]\left\langle\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}},{\bm{v}}\right\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 3\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[1-\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}}\right]\cdot\sup_{t\in[T]}\left\langle\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}},{\bm{v}}\right\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 3\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[1-\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}}\right]\cdot\sup_{t\in[T]}\left\|\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}}^{2}\cdot{\bm{v}}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{T}{\bm{v}}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}3\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[1-\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}}\right]\cdot\sup_{t\in[T]}\left\|\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{t}^{-1}}^{2}\cdot{\bm{v}}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{T}{\bm{v}}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{=}3\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[1-\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}}\right]\cdot\sup_{t\in[T]}\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\cdot{\bm{v}}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{T}{\bm{v}},

where
(a)
uses
{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}\preceq{\bm{H}}_{t}^{-1}
for all
t\in[T]
and
(b)
follows from

\left\|\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{t}^{-1}}^{2}=\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}^{\top}}{\sigma_{t}}\left({\bm{H}}_{t-1}^{-1}-\frac{{\bm{H}}_{t-1}^{-1}\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}^{\top}}{\sigma_{t}}{\bm{H}}_{t-1}^{-1}}{1+\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}^{\top}}{\sigma_{t}}{\bm{H}}_{t-1}^{-1}\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}^{\top}}{\sigma_{t}}}\right)\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}=w_{t}^{2}-\frac{w_{t}^{4}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}=\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}.

By the arbitrariness of
{\bm{v}}
, we know that

{\bm{H}}_{1,T}\preceq 3\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[1-\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}}\right]\cdot\sup_{t\in[T]}\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\cdot{\bm{H}}_{T}.

(B.12)

Let
X_{t}=1-\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}}
.
It is obvious that
0\leq X_{t}\leq 1
.
We then focus on concentration of
\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{t}
.
To that end, we need a variance-aware Bernstein inequality Lemma
F.2
for martingales.
Lemma
F.2
implies that with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{T^{2}}
, we have

\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{t}\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}_{t}X_{t}+3\sqrt{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Var[X_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]\cdot\log\frac{2KT^{2}}{\delta}}+5\log\frac{2KT^{2}}{\delta}

where
K:=1+\lceil 2\log_{2}V\rceil
and
V^{2}
is an upper bound satisfying
\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}[X_{t}^{2}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]\leq V^{2}
.

First notice that for any
t\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}{\mathbb{E}}_{t}X_{t}&=1-{\mathbb{E}}_{t}\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}}={\mathbb{E}}_{t}\frac{z_{t}^{2}({{\bm{\theta}}^{*}})}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}(\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}+\tau_{t})}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{2\tau_{t}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}_{t}z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})\leq\frac{b^{2}}{2\tau_{t}^{2}}\leq\frac{b^{2}}{2\tau_{0}^{2}}\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\end{split}

(B.13)

which implies that

\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}_{t}X_{t}\leq\frac{b^{2}}{2\tau_{0}^{2}}\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\leq\frac{b^{2}}{2\tau_{0}^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\min\{1,w_{t}^{2}\}\leq\frac{\kappa b^{2}}{\tau_{0}^{2}}

where the last inequality uses Lemma
F.5
and thus

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\min\{1,w_{t}^{2}\}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\min\left\{1,\left\|\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}\right\}\leq 2d\log\left(1+\frac{TL^{2}}{d\lambda\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)=2\kappa.

Secondly, we have

\displaystyle\Var[X_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}[X_{t}^{2}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{t}\left(1-\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}}\right)^{2}\overset{(*)}{\leq}\frac{1}{4}\frac{{\mathbb{E}}_{t}z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}{\tau_{t}^{2}}\leq\frac{b^{2}}{4\tau_{t}^{2}}

where
(*)
uses
1-\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}}\leq\frac{z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}{2\tau_{t}\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}}
which is also used in (
B.13
).
As a result, we have

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Var[X_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{b^{2}}{4\tau_{t}^{2}}\leq\frac{b^{2}}{4\tau_{0}^{2}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\leq\frac{\kappa b^{2}}{2\tau_{0}^{2}}.

Once requiring
\tau_{0}^{2}\geq 2\kappa b^{2}
, we have
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Var[X_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]\leq 1
and thus we can set
V=1
and obtain
K=1
.
Putting them together, if
\tau_{0}^{2}\geq\frac{2\kappa b^{2}}{\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}}
, with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\kappa b^{2}}{\tau_{0}^{2}}+\frac{3b}{\tau_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{\kappa\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}}{2}}+5\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}+\frac{3}{2}\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}+5\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}

\displaystyle\leq 9\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}=\frac{1}{12c_{0}^{2}}

(B.14)

where the last equation is due to the definition of
c_{0}
.
Finally, taking a union bound for the last inequality from
T=1
to
\infty
and using the fact that
\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}t^{-2}<2
, we have
\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{t}\leq\frac{1}{12c_{0}^{2}}
for all
T\geq 1
with probability at least
1-2\delta
.

On the other hand, by the choice of
\sigma_{t}
, we have
\sigma_{t}^{2}\geq\frac{1}{c_{0}^{2}}\cdot\|{\bm{\phi}}_{t}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}
, which implies

\sup_{t\in[T]}\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\leq\sup_{t\in[T]}w_{t}^{2}\leq c_{0}^{2}.

(B.15)

Plugging (
B.3
) and (
B.15
) into (
B.12
), we have

{\bm{H}}_{1,T}\preceq\frac{1}{4}{\bm{H}}_{T}.

(B.16)

Analysis of
{\bm{H}}_{2,T}

We first notice that

\displaystyle\left|\left(\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}})}}\right)^{3}-\left(\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}}\right)^{3}\right|\leq 3\left|\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}})}}-\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{3\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}})}\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}}\frac{|z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}})-z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})|}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}})}+\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}}.

(B.17)

Notice that
z_{t}({\bm{\theta}})=z_{t}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})+\langle\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}},{\bm{\theta}}-{\bm{\theta}}^{*}\rangle
.
It then follow that for any
c>0

\displaystyle z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}})\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{c}\right)z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})+(1+c)\left\langle\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}},{\bm{\theta}}-{\bm{\theta}}^{*}\right\rangle^{2};

\displaystyle z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{c}\right)z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}})+(1+c)\left\langle\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}},{\bm{\theta}}-{\bm{\theta}}^{*}\right\rangle^{2},

By discussing which is larger between
z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}})
and
z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})
, we have

|z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}})-z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})|\leq\frac{1}{c}\min\left\{z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}),z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})\right\}+\left(1+c\right)\left\langle\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}},{\bm{\theta}}-{\bm{\theta}}^{*}\right\rangle^{2}.

(B.18)

Plugging (
B.18
) into (
B.3
), we have that

\displaystyle\left|\left(\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}})}}\right)^{3}-\left(\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})}}\right)^{3}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{3\tau_{t}}{\tau_{t}^{2}+\min\left\{z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}),z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})\right\}}\frac{\frac{1}{c}\min\left\{z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}),z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})\right\}}{2\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+\min\left\{z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}),z_{t}^{2}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})\right\}}}+\frac{3(1+c)}{2\tau_{t}^{2}}\left\langle\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}},{\bm{\theta}}-{\bm{\theta}}^{*}\right\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{3}{2c}+\frac{3(1+c)}{2\tau_{t}^{2}}\left\langle\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}},{\bm{\theta}}-{\bm{\theta}}^{*}\right\rangle^{2}\overset{(a)}{\leq}\frac{3}{2c}+\frac{6(1+c)}{\tau_{t}^{2}}\frac{L^{2}B^{2}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{3}{2c}+\frac{6(1+c)}{\tau_{0}^{2}}\frac{w_{t}^{2}L^{2}B^{2}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\overset{(b)}{\leq}\frac{3}{2c}+\frac{6(1+c)c_{1}d}{\tau_{0}^{2}}

(B.19)

where
(a)
uses
\left\langle\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}},{\bm{\theta}}-{\bm{\theta}}^{*}\right\rangle\leq\left\|\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|\left(\|{\bm{\theta}}\|+\|{\bm{\theta}}^{*}\|\right)\leq\frac{2LB}{\sigma_{t}}
due to
\left\|{\bm{\phi}}_{t}\right\|\leq L
and
{\bm{\theta}},{\bm{\theta}}^{*}\in\mathrm{Ball}_{d}(B)
and

(b)
uses the following result.
By the definition of
\sigma_{t}
, we have
\sigma_{t}\geq{\sqrt{LB}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{t}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{{\bm{H}}_{t-1}^{-1}}}/{c_{1}^{\frac{1}{4}}d^{\frac{1}{4}}}
which implies
\sigma_{t}^{2}\geq\frac{w_{t}^{2}L^{2}B^{2}}{c_{1}d}
.
As a result of (
B.3
), by definition of
{\bm{H}}_{3,T}
, we have

-\left(\frac{3}{2c}+\frac{6(1+c)c_{1}d}{\tau_{0}^{2}}\right)\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}^{\top}}{\sigma_{t}}\preceq{\bm{H}}_{2,T}

(B.20)

Putting pieces together

Plugging (
B.16
) and (
B.20
) into (
B.11
), with probability at least
1-\delta
, for any
T\geq 1
and for all
{\bm{\theta}}\in\mathrm{Ball}_{d}(B)
, we have

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}L_{T}({\bm{\theta}})

\displaystyle\succeq{\bm{H}}_{T}-\frac{1}{4}{\bm{H}}_{T}-\left(\frac{3}{2c}+\frac{6(1+c)c_{1}d}{\tau_{0}^{2}}\right)\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}^{\top}}{\sigma_{t}}

\displaystyle\succeq\frac{3\lambda}{4}{\bm{I}}+\left(1-\frac{1}{4}-\frac{3}{2c}-\frac{6(1+c)c_{1}d}{\tau_{0}^{2}}\right)\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}^{\top}}{\sigma_{t}}.

Notice that
c_{1}=\frac{1}{42\cdot\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}}
.
If we set
c=6
and
\tau_{0}\sqrt{\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}}\geq\max\{\sqrt{2\kappa}b,2\sqrt{d}\}
, we have

\max\left\{\frac{3}{2c},\frac{6(1+c)c_{1}d}{\tau_{0}^{2}}\right\}\leq\frac{1}{4}.

As a result, we have

\nabla^{2}L_{T}({\bm{\theta}})\succeq\frac{3\lambda}{4}{\bm{I}}+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{{\bm{\phi}}}{\sigma_{t}}\frac{{\bm{\phi}}^{\top}}{\sigma_{t}}\succeq\frac{1}{4}{\bm{H}}_{T}.

B.4
Proof of Lemma
B.2

For simplicity, we denote
z_{t}^{*}=z_{t}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})
for short.
By triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla L_{T}({\bm{\theta}}^{*})\|_{{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\lambda{\bm{\theta}}^{*}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}}+\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\tau_{t}z_{t}^{*}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}}\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\lambda{\bm{\theta}}^{*}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}}+\underbrace{\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\tau_{t}z_{t}^{*}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}}\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}}}_{:={\bm{d}}_{T}}.

(B.21)

For the residual term
\|\lambda{\bm{\theta}}^{*}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}}

Notice that
{\bm{H}}_{T}\succeq\lambda{\bm{I}}
and thus
{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}\preceq\lambda^{-1}{\bm{I}}_{d}
.
Therefore,
\|\lambda{\bm{\theta}}^{*}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}}\leq\sqrt{\lambda}B
.

For the self-normalized term
\|{\bm{d}}_{T}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}}

The fact that
{\bm{H}}_{T}={\bm{H}}_{T-1}+\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{T}{\bm{\phi}}_{T}^{\top}}{\sigma_{T}^{2}}
together with the Woodbury matrix identity implies that

{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}={\bm{H}}_{T-1}^{-1}-\frac{{\bm{H}}_{T-1}^{-1}{\bm{\phi}}_{T}{\bm{\phi}}_{T}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{T-1}^{-1}}{\sigma_{T}^{2}(1+w_{T}^{2})}\quad\text{where}\quad w_{T}^{2}:=\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{T}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{T-1}^{-1}{\bm{\phi}}_{T}}{\sigma_{T}^{2}}=\left\|\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{T}}{\sigma_{T}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{T-1}}^{2}.

(B.22)

Clearly,
w_{T}
is
{\mathcal{F}}_{T-1}
-measurable and thus is predictable.
By definition of
{\bm{d}}_{T}
and (
B.22
),

\displaystyle\|{\bm{d}}_{T}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left({\bm{d}}_{T-1}+\frac{{{\tau}}_{T}z_{T}^{*}}{\sqrt{{{\tau}}_{T}^{2}+(z_{T}^{*})^{2}}}\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{T}}{\sigma_{T}}\right)^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}\left({\bm{d}}_{T-1}+\frac{{{\tau}}_{T}z_{T}^{*}}{\sqrt{{{\tau}}_{T}^{2}+(z_{T}^{*})^{2}}}\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{T}}{\sigma_{T}}\right)

\displaystyle=\|{\bm{d}}_{T-1}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{T-1}^{-1}}^{2}-\frac{1}{1+w_{T}^{2}}\left(\frac{{\bm{d}}_{T-1}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{T-1}^{-1}{\bm{\phi}}_{T}}{\sigma_{T}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{2{{\tau}}_{T}z_{T}^{*}}{\sqrt{{{\tau}}_{T}^{2}+(z_{T}^{*})^{2}}}\frac{{\bm{d}}_{T-1}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}{\bm{\phi}}_{T}}{\sigma_{T}}+\frac{\tau_{T}^{2}(z_{T}^{*})^{2}}{\tau_{T}^{2}+(z_{T}^{*})^{2}}\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{T}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}{\bm{\phi}}_{T}}{\sigma_{T}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bm{d}}_{T-1}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{T-1}^{-1}}^{2}+\underbrace{\frac{2{{\tau}}_{T}z_{T}^{*}}{\sqrt{{{\tau}}_{T}^{2}+(z_{T}^{*})^{2}}}\frac{{\bm{d}}_{T-1}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}{\bm{\phi}}_{T}}{\sigma_{T}}}_{I_{1}}+\underbrace{\frac{\tau_{T}^{2}(z_{T}^{*})^{2}}{\tau_{T}^{2}+(z_{T}^{*})^{2}}\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{T}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}{\bm{\phi}}_{T}}{\sigma_{T}^{2}}}_{I_{2}}.

(B.23)

For
I_{1}
, by (
B.22
), we have

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{2{{\tau}}_{T}z_{T}^{*}}{\sqrt{{{\tau}}_{T}^{2}+(z_{T}^{*})^{2}}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{T}}{\bm{d}}_{T-1}^{\top}\left({\bm{H}}_{T-1}^{-1}-\frac{{\bm{H}}_{T-1}^{-1}{\bm{\phi}}_{T}{\bm{\phi}}_{T}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{T-1}^{-1}}{\sigma_{T}^{2}(1+w_{T}^{2})}\right){\bm{\phi}}_{T}

\displaystyle=\frac{2{{\tau}}_{T}z_{T}^{*}}{\sqrt{{{\tau}}_{T}^{2}+(z_{T}^{*})^{2}}}\frac{1}{1+w_{T}^{2}}\frac{{\bm{d}}_{T-1}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{T-1}^{-1}{\bm{\phi}}_{T}}{\sigma_{T}}.

For
I_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{{{\tau}}_{T}^{2}(z_{T}^{*})^{2}}{{{\tau}}_{T}^{2}+(z_{T}^{*})^{2}}\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{T}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}{\bm{\phi}}_{T}}{\sigma_{T}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{{{\tau}}_{T}^{2}(z_{T}^{*})^{2}}{{{\tau}}_{T}^{2}+(z_{T}^{*})^{2}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{T}^{2}}{\bm{\phi}}_{T}^{\top}\left({\bm{H}}_{T-1}^{-1}-\frac{{\bm{H}}_{T-1}^{-1}{\bm{\phi}}_{T}{\bm{\phi}}_{T}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{T-1}^{-1}}{\sigma_{T}^{2}(1+w_{T}^{2})}\right){\bm{\phi}}_{T}

\displaystyle=\frac{{{\tau}}_{T}^{2}(z_{T}^{*})^{2}}{{{\tau}}_{T}^{2}+(z_{T}^{*})^{2}}\left(w_{T}^{2}-\frac{w_{T}^{4}}{1+w_{T}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{{{\tau}}_{T}^{2}(z_{T}^{*})^{2}}{{{\tau}}_{T}^{2}+(z_{T}^{*})^{2}}\frac{w_{T}^{2}}{1+w_{T}^{2}}.

Using the equations for
I_{1},I_{2}
and iterating (
B.4
), we have

\|{\bm{d}}_{T}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}}^{2}\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{{{\tau}}_{t}z_{t}^{*}}{\sqrt{{{\tau}}_{t}^{2}+(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}}\frac{2}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\frac{{\bm{d}}_{t-1}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{t-1}^{-1}{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{{{\tau}}_{t}^{2}(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}{{{\tau}}_{t}^{2}+(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}.

Recall that

\kappa=d\log\left(1+\frac{TL^{2}}{d\lambda\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right).

Lemma B.3
.

Assume
{\mathbb{E}}[(z_{t}^{*})^{2}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]\leq b^{2}
for all
t\geq 1
.
Let
A_{t}
denotes the event where
\|{\bm{d}}_{n}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{n}^{-1}}\leq\alpha_{n}
for all
n\in[t]
.
With probability at least
1-\delta/2
, we have for all
T\geq 1
,

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{2\tau_{t}z_{t}^{*}{\bm{1}}_{A_{t-1}}}{(\tau_{t}^{2}+(z_{t}^{*})^{2})^{1/2}}\frac{1}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\frac{{\bm{d}}_{t-1}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{t-1}^{-1}{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}\leq 4\max_{t\in[T]}\alpha_{t}\cdot\left[\frac{\kappa b^{2}}{4\tau_{0}}+b\sqrt{\kappa\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}}+\frac{2\tau_{0}}{3}\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}\right].

Lemma B.4
.

Assume
{\mathbb{E}}[(z_{t}^{*})^{2}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]\leq b^{2}
for all
t\geq 1
.
For a fixed
\tau\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-\delta/2
, the follow inequality uniformly holds for all
T\geq 1
,

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\tau_{t}^{2}(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}{\tau_{t}^{2}+(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\leq\left[\sqrt{2\kappa}b+\tau_{0}\sqrt{\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}}\right]^{2}.

For any
T\geq 1
, we define

\alpha_{T}=8\left[\frac{\kappa b^{2}}{\tau_{0}}+b\sqrt{\kappa\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}}+\tau_{0}\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}\right].

(B.24)

As a result of Lemma
B.3
and Lemma
B.4
, with probability at least
1-\delta
, for all
T\geq 1
,

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{2\tau_{t}z_{t}^{*}{\bm{1}}_{A_{t-1}}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}}\frac{1}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\frac{{\bm{d}}_{t-1}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{t-1}^{-1}{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}\leq\frac{\alpha_{T}^{2}}{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{\tau_{t}^{2}(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}{\tau_{t}^{2}+(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\leq\frac{\alpha_{T}^{2}}{2}.

We can conclude that all
\{A_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
is true and thus
\|{\bm{d}}_{T}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{T}^{-1}}\leq\alpha_{T}
for all
T\geq 1
.

B.5
Proof of Lemma
B.3

Proof of Lemma
B.3
.

We will make use of the Freedman inequality Lemma
F.1
to prove our result. Recall that
\tau_{t}=\tau_{0}\frac{\sqrt{1+w_{t}^{2}}}{w_{t}}
.
Set
Y_{t}=\frac{\tau_{t}z_{t}^{*}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}}\frac{2}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\frac{{\bm{d}}_{t-1}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{t-1}^{-1}{\bm{\phi}}_{t}{\bm{1}}_{A_{t-1}}}{\sigma_{t}}
with the event
A_{t-1}
defined in the lemma.
For simplicity, we denote
X_{t}=Y_{t}-{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]
.
Notice that

\left|\frac{{\bm{d}}_{t-1}^{\top}{\bm{H}}_{t-1}^{-1}{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}\cdot{\bm{1}}_{A_{t-1}}\right|\leq\|{\bm{d}}_{t-1}{\bm{1}}_{A_{t-1}}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{t-1}^{-1}}\cdot\left\|\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{t}}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{t-1}^{-1}}\leq\alpha_{t-1}w_{t}.

As a result, we have

|Y_{t}|\leq\tau_{t}\alpha_{t-1}\cdot\frac{2w_{t}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\leq 2\tau_{0}\alpha_{t-1}\quad\text{and thus}\quad|X_{t}|\leq|Y_{t}|+|{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]|\leq 4\tau_{0}\alpha_{t-1}.

We also find that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[X_{t}^{2}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{t}^{2}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]={\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(\frac{2w_{t}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\right)^{2}\|{\bm{d}}_{t-1}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{t-1}^{-1}}^{2}{\bm{1}}_{A_{t-1}}\frac{\tau_{t}^{2}(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}{\tau_{t}^{2}+(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}\bigg|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}\left(\frac{2w_{t}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\right)^{2}\alpha_{t-1}^{2}b^{2}\leq\min\{1,2w_{t}\}^{2}\alpha_{t-1}^{2}b^{2}\leq 4\min\{1,w_{t}^{2}\}\alpha_{t-1}^{2}b^{2}

where
(a)
uses
{\mathbb{E}}(X-{\mathbb{E}}X)^{2}\leq{\mathbb{E}}X^{2}
for any random variable
X
and
(b)
uses
{\mathbb{E}}[\varepsilon_{t}^{2}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]\leq b^{2}\sigma_{t}^{2}
due to
{\mathbb{E}}[(z_{t}^{*})^{2}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]\leq b^{2}
.

Notice that
\|{\bm{\phi}}_{t}\|/\sigma_{t}\leq\|{\bm{\phi}}_{t}\|/\sigma_{\min}\leq L/\sigma_{\min}
.
Then by Lemma
F.5
, we have

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\min\{1,w_{t}^{2}\}\leq 2d\log\left(1+\frac{TL^{2}}{d\lambda\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right):=2\kappa.

(B.25)

Hence, by (
B.25
),

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}[X_{t}^{2}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{t=1}^{T}\min\{1,w_{t}^{2}\}\alpha_{t-1}^{2}b^{2}\leq 4\max_{t\in[T]}\alpha_{t}^{2}\cdot\sum_{t=1}^{T}\min\{1,w_{t}^{2}\}b^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{t\in[T]}\alpha_{t}^{2}\cdot 8db^{2}\log\left(1+\frac{TL^{2}}{d\lambda\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)\leq 8\kappa b^{2}\cdot\max_{t\in[T]}\alpha_{t}^{2}.

On the other hand, using
{\mathbb{E}}[z_{t}^{*}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]=0
we have

\displaystyle\left|{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{\tau_{t}z_{t}^{*}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}}\bigg|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}\right]\right|=\left|{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(\frac{\tau_{t}}{\sqrt{\tau_{t}^{2}+(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}}-1\right)z_{t}^{*}\bigg|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}\right]\right|\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}{2\tau_{t}}\bigg|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}\right]\leq\frac{b^{2}}{2\tau_{t}}

which implies

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{b^{2}}{2\tau_{t}}\frac{w_{t}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\alpha_{t-1}\leq\frac{b^{2}}{2\tau_{0}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\alpha_{t-1}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{t\in[T]}\alpha_{t}\cdot\frac{b^{2}}{2\tau}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\min\{1,w_{t}^{2}\}\leq\sup_{t\in[T]}\alpha_{t}\cdot\frac{\kappa b^{2}}{\tau_{0}}.

By Freedman inequality in Lemma
F.1
, it follows that for a given
T
and
\tau_{0}
, with probability
1-\frac{\delta}{2T^{2}}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]\right|+4\max_{t\in[T]}\alpha_{t}\cdot\left[b\sqrt{\kappa\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}}+\frac{2\tau_{0}}{3}\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4\max_{t\in[T]}\alpha_{t}\cdot\left[\frac{\kappa b^{2}}{4\tau_{0}}+b\sqrt{\kappa\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}}+\frac{2\tau_{0}}{3}\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}\right].

Finally, taking a union bound for the last inequality from
T=1
to
\infty
and using the fact that
\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}t^{-2}<2
complete the proof.
∎

B.6
Proof of Lemma
B.4

Proof of Lemma
B.4
.

Set
Y_{t}=\frac{\tau_{t}^{2}(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}{\tau_{t}^{2}+(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}
and
X_{t}=Y_{t}-{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]
.
Recall that
\tau_{t}=\tau_{0}\frac{\sqrt{1+w_{t}^{2}}}{w_{t}}
.
Clearly, we have
|Y_{t}|\leq\tau_{t}^{2}\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\leq\tau_{0}^{2}
and thus
|X_{t}|=|Y_{t}-{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]|\leq\max\{|Y_{t}|,|{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]|\}\leq\tau_{0}^{2}
.
We also find that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[X_{t}^{2}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{t}^{2}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]\leq\left(\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\right)^{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left(\frac{\tau_{t}^{2}(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}{\tau_{t}^{2}+(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}\right)^{2}\bigg|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\tau_{t}^{2}\left(\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\right)^{2}{\mathbb{E}}\left[(z_{t}^{*})^{2}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}\right]\overset{(b)}{\leq}\tau_{0}^{2}b^{2}\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}

where
(a)
uses
{\mathbb{E}}(X-{\mathbb{E}}X)^{2}\leq{\mathbb{E}}X^{2}
for any random variable
X
and
(b)
uses
{\mathbb{E}}[(z_{t}^{*})^{2}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]\leq b^{2}
due to
{\mathbb{E}}[\varepsilon_{t}^{2}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]\leq b^{2}\nu_{t}^{2}
.
Hence, by (
B.25
), we have

\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}[X_{t}^{2}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]\leq\tau_{0}^{2}b^{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}\leq\tau_{0}^{2}b^{2}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\min\{1,w_{t}^{2}\}\leq 2\kappa\tau_{0}^{2}b^{2}.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{\tau_{t}^{2}(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}{\tau_{t}^{2}+(z_{t}^{*})^{2}}\bigg|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\frac{w_{t}^{2}}{1+w_{t}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[(z_{t}^{*})^{2}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}\right]\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\min\{1,w_{t}^{2}\}b^{2}\leq 2\kappa b^{2}.

By Lemma
F.1
, it follows that with probability
1-\frac{\delta}{2T^{2}}
,

\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{t}|{\mathcal{F}}_{t-1}]+2\tau_{0}b\sqrt{\kappa\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}}+\frac{2\tau_{0}^{2}}{3}\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}

for a given
T
and
\tau_{0}
.
Putting all pieces together, it follows that with probability
1-\frac{\delta}{2T^{2}}
,

\sum_{t=1}^{T}Y_{t}\leq\left[\sqrt{2\kappa}b+\tau_{0}\sqrt{\log\frac{2T^{2}}{\delta}}\right]^{2}.

Finally, taking a union bound for the last inequality from
T=1
to
\infty
and using the fact that
\sum_{t=1}^{\infty}t^{-2}<2
complete the proof.

∎

Appendix C
Proof of Theorem
4.1

Measurability

Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{h,k}
denote the
\sigma
-field generated by all random variables up tp and including the
h
-th step and
k
-th episode.
More specifically, let
I_{h,k}=\{(i,j):i\in[H],j\in[k-1]\ \text{or}\ i\in[h],j=k\}
denote the set of index pairs up to and including the
h
-th step and
k
-th episode and then
{\mathcal{F}}_{h,k}=\sigma\left(\cup_{(i,j)\in I_{h,k}}\left\{s_{i,j},a_{i,j},r_{i,j}\right\}\right).

We make a convention that
{\mathcal{F}}_{0,k}={\mathcal{F}}_{H,k-1}
.
From our algorithm, we know that (i)
Q_{h}^{k},V_{h}^{k},\pi_{h}^{k}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{H,k-1}
for any
Q\in\{\overline{Q},\widehat{Q},\widecheck{Q},\underline{Q}\}
and
V\in\{\overline{V},\widehat{V},\widecheck{V},\underline{V}\}
, and (ii)

{\bm{\mu}}_{h-1,k},{\bm{\theta}}_{h,k},{\bm{\psi}}_{h,k},\sigma_{h,k},U_{h,k},J_{h,k},E_{h,k},{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k},\widetilde{{\bm{\phi}}}_{h,k},w_{h,k},\widetilde{w}_{h,k},\tau_{h,k},\widetilde{\tau}_{h,k},{\bm{H}}_{h,k},\widetilde{{\bm{H}}}_{h,k}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{h,k}.

C.1
High-Probability Events

Let
\kappa=d\log\left(1+\frac{K}{d\lambda\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)
.
We first introduce the following high-probability events.

1.

We define
{\mathcal{B}}_{R^{2}}
as the event that the following inequalities hold for all
h\in[H]
and
k\in[K]\cup\{0\}
,

{\bm{\psi}}_{h}^{*}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{h,k}:=\left\{\|{\bm{\psi}}\|\leq W:\|{\bm{\psi}}_{h,k}-{\bm{\psi}}\|_{\widetilde{{\bm{H}}}_{h,k}^{-1}}\leq\beta_{R^{2}}\right\}

where

\beta_{R^{2}}=128\left(\frac{\sqrt{\kappa}\sigma_{R^{2}}}{\sigma_{\min}}+\sqrt{d}\right)\sqrt{\log\frac{2HK^{2}}{\delta}}+5\sqrt{\lambda}W.

2.

We define
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
as the event that the following inequalities hold for all
h\in[H]
and
k\in[K]
,

\displaystyle\max\left\{\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1})\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}},\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1})\underline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}\right\}\leq\beta_{0},

\displaystyle\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1})[\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}]^{2}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}\leq{\mathcal{H}}\beta_{0}

where

\displaystyle\beta_{0}=\frac{4{\mathcal{H}}}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{d^{3}H\iota_{0}^{2}+\log\frac{2H}{\delta}}+3\sqrt{d\lambda}{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\iota_{0}=\max\left\{\log\left(1+\frac{8LK}{\lambda{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{d}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right),\log\left(1+\frac{32B^{2}K^{2}}{\sqrt{d}\lambda^{3}{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\sigma_{\min}^{4}}\right),\log\left(1+\frac{K}{\lambda\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)\right\}.

(C.1)

Here we choose
B\geq 3(\beta_{R}+\beta_{V})
and
L=W+{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\frac{dK}{\lambda}}
.

3.

We define
{\mathcal{B}}_{R}
as the event that the following inequalities hold for all
h\in[H]
and
k\in[K]\cup\{0\}
,

\displaystyle{\bm{\theta}}_{h}^{*}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{h,k}:=\left\{\|{\bm{\theta}}\|\leq W:\|{\bm{\theta}}_{h,k}-{\bm{\theta}}_{h}^{*}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k}}\leq\beta_{R}\right\},

\displaystyle\left|[\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{h}\widehat{R}_{h}-{\mathbb{V}}_{h}R_{h}](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\right|\leq R_{h,k}:=\beta_{R^{2}}\|\widetilde{{\bm{\phi}}}_{h,k}\|_{\widetilde{{\bm{H}}}_{h,k-1}^{k-1}}+2{\mathcal{H}}\beta_{R}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}},

(
4.8
)

where

\displaystyle\beta_{R}=128(\sqrt{\kappa}+\sqrt{d})\sqrt{\log\frac{2HK^{2}}{\delta}}+5\sqrt{\lambda}W.

4.

We define
{\mathcal{B}}_{h}
as the event such that for all episode
k\in[K]
, all stages
h\leq h^{\prime}\leq H
,

\displaystyle\max\left\{\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h^{\prime}}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h^{\prime},k-1})\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h^{\prime}+1}^{k}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h^{\prime},k-1}},\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h^{\prime}}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h^{\prime},k-1})\underline{{\bm{V}}}_{h^{\prime}+1}^{k}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h^{\prime},k-1}}\right\}\leq\beta_{V}

(C.2)

where

\displaystyle\beta_{V}={\mathcal{O}}\left(\sqrt{d}\iota_{1}^{2}+\sqrt{d\lambda}{\mathcal{H}}\right)

\displaystyle\iota_{1}=\max\left\{\iota_{0},\log\frac{4HK^{2}}{\delta},\log\left(1+\frac{4L\sqrt{d^{3}H}}{\sigma_{\min}}\right),\log\left(1+\frac{8\sqrt{d^{7}}HB^{2}}{\lambda\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)\right\}.

(C.3)

For simplicity, we further define
{\mathcal{B}}_{V}:={\mathcal{B}}_{1}
.

Our ultimate goal is to show
{\mathcal{B}}_{V}
holds with high probability, a target used in previous work
[
Hu et al. 2022
,
He et al. 2022
]
.
More specifically, we first obtain coarse confidence sets for all parameters in the sense that the confidence radius (that is
\beta_{R^{2}}
and
\beta_{0}
) is loose.
In our analysis,
{\mathcal{B}}_{R^{2}}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
serves as the ‘coarse’ event where the concentration results hold with a larger confidence radius, and
{\mathcal{B}}_{R}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{V}
serves as a ‘refined’ event where the confidence radius (that is
\beta_{R}
and
\beta_{V}
) is much is tighter.
Our first result is that
{\mathcal{B}}_{R^{2}}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
holds with high probability as shown in Lemma
C.1
and
C.2
.
Their proofs are collected in Appendix
E.1
and
E.2
.

Lemma C.1
.

If we set

\widetilde{\tau}_{0}=\max\left\{\frac{\sqrt{2\kappa}\sigma_{R^{2}}}{\sigma_{\min}},2\sqrt{d}\right\}\bigg/\sqrt{\log\frac{2HK^{2}}{\delta}},

(C.4)

the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{R^{2}}
holds with probability at least
1-3\delta
.

Lemma C.2
.

The event
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
holds with probability at least
1-3\delta
.

These coarse confidence sets are then used to estimate variance for the reward functions and value functions.
A key step is to show the adapted variance
\sigma_{h,k}
’s are indeed upper bounds of these variances (that is
[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}R_{h}](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})+[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}V_{h+1}^{*}](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})
) for all
h\in[H]
.
A frequently used argument is backward induction.
That is given the estimation is optimistic at the stage
h+1
, we then show the optimistic estimation is maintained at the stage
h
.
Induction over the stage
h
would complete the proof.
The following lemma provides estimation error bounds for
[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}R_{h}](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})
and shows that the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{R}
holds with high probability.
Its proof is deferred in Appendix
E.4
.

Lemma C.3
.

If we set

\tau_{0}=\max\{\sqrt{2\kappa},2\sqrt{d}\}\bigg/\sqrt{\log\frac{2HK^{2}}{\delta}},

(C.5)

the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{R}
holds with probability at least
1-6\delta
.

In Lemma
C.4
, we show that that our constructed value functions
\overline{V}
and
\underline{V}
are indeed optimistic and pessimistic estimators of the true value functions under the event defined before.
Its proof is deferred in Appendix
E.5
.

Lemma C.4

(Optimism and pessimism)
.

For any
h\in[H]
, if
{\mathcal{B}}_{R}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{h}
holds, for any
k\in[K]\cup\{0\}
,

\underline{V}_{h}^{k}(\cdot)\leq V_{h}^{*}(\cdot)\leq\overline{V}_{h}^{k}(\cdot).

With the established optimism and pessimism, we can establish upper bounds for the estimation errors of the three terms, namely
[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}V_{h+1}^{*}](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})
,
\left[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}(\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-V_{h+1}^{*})\right](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})
, and
\left[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}(\underline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-V_{h+1}^{*})\right](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})
in the following lemmas.
Their proofs are deferred in Appendix
E.6
and
E.7
.

Lemma C.5
.

On the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{h+1}
, it follows that for all
k\in[K]

\left|\left[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}V_{h+1}^{*}-\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{h}\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}\right](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\right|\leq U_{h,k}

where

U_{h,k}=\min\left\{{\mathcal{V}}^{2},11{\mathcal{H}}\beta_{0}\cdot\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}+4{\mathcal{H}}\cdot\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{h,k}(\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-\underline{V}_{h+1}^{k})(s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\right\}

(
4.9
)

with
\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{h,k}(\cdot|s,a)={\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}^{\top}{\bm{\phi}}(s,a)
.

Lemma C.6
.

On the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{R}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{h+1}
, it follows that for all
j\leq k\leq K

\max\left\{\left[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}(\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-V_{h+1}^{*})\right](s_{h,j},a_{h,j}),\left[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}(\underline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-V_{h+1}^{*})\right](s_{h,j},a_{h,j})\right\}\leq E_{h,j}

where

E_{h,j}=\min\left\{{\mathcal{H}}^{2},2{\mathcal{H}}\beta_{0}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,j-1}^{-1}}+{\mathcal{H}}\cdot\left[\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{h,j}(\overline{V}_{h+1}^{j}-\underline{V}_{h+1}^{j})\right](s_{h,j},a_{h,j})\right\}

(
4.7
)

with
\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{h,j}(\cdot|s,a)={\bm{\mu}}_{h,j-1}^{\top}{\bm{\phi}}(s,a)
.

With the last four lemmas, one can easily prove
\sigma_{h,k}
indeed serves as an upper bound of the true variance of
V_{h+1}^{*}
at stage
h
.
Therefore, by the backward induction, we can prove the following lemma whose proof is in Appendix
E.8
.

Lemma C.7
.

On the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{R}
, the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{V}
holds with probability at least
1-2\delta
.

C.2
Regret Analysis

In the previous subsection, we know that with probability at least
1-15\delta
, the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{V}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{R}
holds.
Based on Lemma
C.4
, the optimism implies that

\mathrm{Reg}(K):=\sum_{k=1}^{K}(V_{1}^{*}-V_{1}^{\pi_{k}})(s_{1,k})\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}(\overline{V}_{1}^{k}-V_{1}^{\pi_{k}})(s_{1,k}).

We then relate the suboptimality gap
\sum_{k=1}^{k}(\overline{V}_{1}^{k}-V_{1}^{\pi_{k}})(s_{1,k})
to the term
\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}
in Lemma
C.8
.
We emphasize that the bound in Lemma
C.8
is much finer than previous bounds (e.g., Lemma B.1 in
[
He et al. 2022
]
) in the sense that the rest term is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(H{\mathcal{H}})
instead of previous
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\sqrt{HK}{\mathcal{H}})
.
This is because we adapt a variance-aware Berinstain inequality to relate the variance of
\sum_{k=1}^{k}(\overline{V}_{1}^{k}-V_{1}^{\pi_{k}})(s_{1,k})
with its expectations and use a recursion argument to simplify the final expression, while previous work directly apply Azuma-Hoeffding inequality to analyze the concentration of
\sum_{k=1}^{k}(\overline{V}_{1}^{k}-V_{1}^{\pi_{k}})(s_{1,k})
, which inevitably introduces the additional
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\sqrt{K})
dependence.
Its proof is collected in Appendix
E.9
.

Lemma C.8

(Suboptimality gap)
.

With probability at least
1-\delta
, on the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{R}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{V}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}(\overline{V}_{1}^{k}-V_{1}^{\pi_{k}})(s_{1,k})\leq 6\beta\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}+38H{\mathcal{H}}\log\frac{4\lceil\log_{2}HK\rceil}{\delta}\ \text{and}\

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}{\mathbb{P}}_{h}(\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-V_{h+1}^{\pi_{k}})(s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\leq 8H\beta\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}+38H^{2}{\mathcal{H}}\log\frac{4\lceil\log_{2}HK\rceil}{\delta}.

Using a similar argument, we provide a finer bound for the gap between optimistic and pessimistic value functions
\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}{\mathbb{P}}_{h}(\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-\underline{V}_{h+1}^{k})(s_{h,k},a_{h,k})
in Lemma
C.9
.
Its proof is provided in Appendix
E.10
.

Lemma C.9

(Gap between optimistic and pessimistic value functions)
.

With probability at least
1-\delta
, on the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{V}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{R}
, it follows that

\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}{\mathbb{P}}_{h}(\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-\underline{V}_{h+1}^{k})(s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\leq 12H\beta\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}+38H^{2}{\mathcal{H}}\log\frac{4\lceil\log_{2}HK\rceil}{\delta}.

The following issue is to upper bound the term
\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}
.
Since the estimation of reward variance concerns the other term
\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\|\widetilde{{\bm{\phi}}}_{h,k}\|_{\widetilde{{\bm{H}}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}
, we are motivated to analyze them simultaneously via
\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}b_{h,k}
where
b_{h,k}=\max\left\{\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}},\|\widetilde{{\bm{\phi}}}_{h,k}\|_{\widetilde{{\bm{H}}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}\right\}
.
Previous works
[
Hu et al. 2022
,
He et al. 2022
]
mainly use Cauchy–Schwarz inequality to analyze it and obtain

\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}b_{h,k}\leq\sqrt{\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sigma_{h,k}^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\max\{w_{h,k}^{2},\widetilde{w}_{h,k}^{2}\}\right)}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\sqrt{dH}\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sigma_{h,k}^{2}}\right).

where the last equality uses the elliptical potential lemmas in Lemma
F.5
.
A standard analysis of the law of total variation would imply
\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\sigma_{h,k}^{2}}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\sqrt{H^{2}K})
.
However, this result doesn’t satisfy our target for two reasons.
First, due to the use of adaptive Huber regression, our definition of
\sigma_{h,k}
is more complicated than previous algorithms.
We need a more elaborate analysis to handle the additional terms in the definition of
\sigma_{h,k}
’s.
Second, the previous result considers the worst-case scenario, while our target is to provide a finer variance-aware regret.
Therefore, it is imperative to provide a finer bound for the sum of bonuses
\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}b_{h,k}
.
We did it in Lemma
C.10
.

Lemma C.10

(Sum of bonuses)
.

Set
\lambda=\frac{1}{{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+W^{2}}
.
Let
{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
denote the intersection event of Lemma
C.8
and
C.9
.
With probability at least
1-2\delta
, on the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{R}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{V}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{R^{2}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}b_{h,k}=

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\sqrt{dHK{\mathcal{G}}^{*}}+Hd^{0.5}K^{0.5}\sigma_{\min}+\frac{H^{2.5}d^{5.5}{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+Hd^{1.5}\sigma_{R^{2}}}{\sigma_{\min}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(+H^{3}d^{4.5}{\mathcal{H}}+Hd^{0.5}\sigma_{R}+Hd^{1.5}\right).

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\cdot)
ignores constant factors and logarithmic dependence.

We emphasize that Lemma
C.10
is perhaps the most technical lemma in our paper.
To address the difficulty mentioned early, we divide the full index set
{\mathcal{I}}:=[H]\times[K]
into three disjoint subsets
{\mathcal{I}}=\cup_{i=1,2,3}{\mathcal{J}}_{i}
according to which value
\sigma_{h,k}
takes (given
\sigma_{h,k}
is the maximum value among five quantities).
For those indexes in
{\mathcal{J}}_{1}
where the bonuses are small enough, we still use the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality to bound
\sum_{(h,k)\in{\mathcal{J}}_{1}}b_{h,k}\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(\sqrt{dH}\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{(h,k)\in{\mathcal{I}}}\sigma_{h,k}^{2}}\right)
.
This sum-of-squared-bonus quantity involves
\sum_{(h,k)\in{\mathcal{I}}}E_{h,k}
and
\sum_{(h,k)\in{\mathcal{I}}}J_{h,k}
which we then pay additional efforts to analyze.
For those indexes in
{\mathcal{J}}_{2}
or
{\mathcal{J}}_{3}
where the bonuses are relatively large, we directly analyze
\sum_{(h,k)\in{\mathcal{J}}_{2}\cup{\mathcal{J}}_{3}}b_{h,k}
.
Thanks to the particular structure,
\sum_{(h,k)\in{\mathcal{J}}_{2}\cup{\mathcal{J}}_{3}}b_{h,k}
contributes to the non-leading term in the final bound.
Putting pieces together, we complete the proof. A formal proof can be found in Appendix
E.11
.

At the end of the subsection, we summarize the proof in a few lines.

\displaystyle\mathrm{Reg}(K)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}(V_{1}^{*}-V_{1}^{\pi_{k}})(s_{1,k})\overset{(a)}{\leq}\sum_{k=1}^{K}(\overline{V}_{1}^{k}-V_{1}^{\pi_{k}})(s_{1,k})

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}3\beta\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}+38H{\mathcal{H}}\log\frac{4\lceil\log_{2}HK\rceil}{\delta}

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}3\beta\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{h=1}^{H}b_{h,k}+38H{\mathcal{H}}\log\frac{4\lceil\log_{2}HK\rceil}{\delta}

\displaystyle\overset{(d)}{=}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(d\sqrt{HK{\mathcal{G}}^{*}}+HdK^{0.5}\sigma_{\min}+\frac{H^{2.5}d^{6}{\mathcal{H}}^{2}+Hd^{2}\sigma_{R^{2}}}{\sigma_{\min}}+H^{3}d^{5}{\mathcal{H}}+Hd\sigma_{R}+Hd^{2}\right)

where
(a)
follows from the optimism result in Lemma
C.4
,
(b)
follows from the suboptimality gap result in Lemma
C.8
,
(c)
uses
b_{h,k}=\max\left\{\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}},\|\widetilde{{\bm{\phi}}}_{h,k}\|_{\widetilde{{\bm{H}}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}\right\}
, and
(d)
follows from sum-of-bonus result in Lemma
C.10
and
\beta=\beta_{R}+\beta_{V}=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\sqrt{d})
.

Appendix D
Proof of Theorem
4.2

Proof of Theorem
4.2
.

We consider the two complexities respectively.

Space Complexity

First, in order to perform AdaOFUL, VARA needs to store all seen rewards and feature vectors (i.e.,
{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k},\widetilde{{\bm{\phi}}}_{h,k}
), which is required by all RL/bandit algorithms robust to heavy-tailed rewards
[
Shao et al. 2018
,
Xue et al. 2021
,
Zhuang and Sui 2021
]
.
AdaOFUL also keeps all robustification parameters
\tau_{h,k},\widetilde{\tau}_{h,k}
.
It then incurs
{\mathcal{O}}(HKd)
space storage in total.

Second, due to the rare-switching technique, one can show that
\overline{Q}_{h}^{k}
(or
\underline{Q}_{h}^{k}
) is the minimum (or maximum) of at most
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(dH)
temporary optimistic (or pessimistic) functions (see Lemma
F.7
).
It means that we need to store at most
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(dH)
different versions of
{\bm{\theta}}_{h,k-1},{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}{\bm{V}}_{h+1}^{k},{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}
’s.
This incurs
{\mathcal{O}}(d^{3}H^{2})
space cost.

Last, for all
(h,k)\in[H]\times[K]
, we need to trace
\{{\bm{\phi}}(s_{h,k},a)\}_{a\in{\mathcal{A}}}
to evaluate each
{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k}{\bm{V}}={\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{h,j}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}V(s_{h+1,k})
for
{\bm{V}}\in\{\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k},[\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}]^{2},\underline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\}
, which takes
{\mathcal{O}}(d|{\mathcal{A}}|HK)
space.

To sum up, VARA takes
{\mathcal{O}}(d^{3}H^{2}+d|{\mathcal{A}}|HK)
space.

Computational Complexity

First, we use Nesterov accelerated method to compute each
{\bm{\theta}}_{h,k}
.
Since the loss function in (
4.1
) is
\lambda
-strongly convex and
\left(\lambda+\frac{K}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)
-smooth, the computational cost for each
{\bm{\theta}}_{h,k}
is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\left(d\sqrt{1+\frac{K}{\lambda(\sigma_{\min}^{*})^{2}}}\right)=\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\max\{d,H^{-3/4}d^{-3/2}K^{3/4}\})
and the total cost is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(HK(d+H^{-3/4}d^{-3/2}K^{3/4}))
.
We emphasize that we don’t need to compute
{\bm{\theta}}_{h,k}
exactly.
It suffices to terminate at a solution
\widehat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{h,k}
once its accuracy satisfies
\|\widehat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{h,k}-{\bm{\theta}}_{h,k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k}}\leq\sqrt{d}
.
The iteration complexity is proportional to the root of the conditional number, i.e.,
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\max\{1,d^{-7/4}K^{3/4}\})
.
Since each iteration takes
{\mathcal{O}}(d)
operation, the computation complexity is
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(\max\{d,d^{-3/4}K^{3/4}\})
.

Second, each time when updating the value function, we take the minimum over at most
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(dH)
quadratic functions.
Moreover, the Sherman-Morrison formula computes
{\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}
and its products with any vectors, which takes
{\mathcal{O}}(d^{2})
operations.
As a result, it needs
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{3}H)
to evaluate the updated
Q_{h,k}(s,a)
for a given pair
(s,a)
.
Hence, computing
Q_{h,k}(s_{h,k},\cdot)
, choosing
a_{h,k}=\mathop{\rm argmax}_{a\in{\mathcal{A}}}Q_{h,k}(s_{h,k},a)
, and estimating the variance
\sigma_{h,k}
lead to
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{3}H^{2}|{\mathcal{A}}|)
computational complexity for each episode.

Last, note
{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k}{\bm{V}}={\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{h,j}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}V(s_{h+1,k})
for any value function
V(\cdot)
.
If
V
remains unchanged, we only need to compute the new term
\sigma_{h,k}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}V(s_{h+1,k})
, which has an
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{3}H|{\mathcal{A}}|)
complexity each time.
If
V
changes to
V^{\prime}
, we need to recalculate
{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k}{\bm{V}}^{\prime}
, which has an
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{3}H|{\mathcal{A}}|K)
complexity each time.
Combining the computational complexity for all horizons and noticing that the number of episodes that trigger the updating criterion is at most
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(dH)
, VARA has a running time of
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{4}|{\mathcal{A}}|H^{3}K+HK(d+H^{-3/4}d^{-3/2}K^{3/4}))
.
In terms of the dependence on
K
, it is slightly worse than LSVI-UCB++’s
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{4}|{\mathcal{A}}|H^{3}K)
since the adaptive Huber regression doesn’t have a closed-form solution, but is better than LSVI-UCB’s
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}(d^{2}|{\mathcal{A}}|HK^{2})
due to the rare-switching mechanism.
∎

Appendix E
Omitted lemmas in Section
C

E.1
Proof of Lemma
C.1

Proof of Lemma
C.1
.

The proof idea of Lemma
C.1
is similar to that of Theorem
3.1
except for the following changes.
First,
\widetilde{{\bm{\phi}}}_{h,k}=\widetilde{{\bm{\phi}}}(s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
is instead the feature vector.
Second, in the particular setting, we should respectively replace
L,B,T,\delta
therein with
1,W,K,\delta/H
defined here and redefine
c_{0},c_{1}
as
c_{0}=\frac{1}{6\sqrt{3\log\frac{2HK^{2}}{\delta}}},c_{1}=\frac{1}{42\cdot\frac{2HK^{2}}{\delta}}
respectively.
Third, by the choice of
\sigma_{h,k}
, we have
\sigma_{h,k}^{2}\geq\left(\frac{W}{\sqrt{c_{1}d}}+{\mathcal{H}}d^{2.5}H\right)b_{h,k}\geq\frac{W}{\sqrt{c_{1}d}}{\|\widetilde{{\bm{\phi}}}_{h,k}\|}_{\widetilde{{\bm{H}}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}
, which implies that
\frac{W^{2}\widetilde{w}_{h,k}^{2}}{\sigma_{h,k}^{2}}\leq c_{1}d
.
Similarly, due to
\sigma_{h,k}^{2}\geq c_{0}^{-2}\|\widetilde{{\bm{\phi}}}_{h,k}\|^{2}_{\widetilde{{\bm{H}}}_{h-1,k}^{-1}}
, we have
\widetilde{w}_{h,k}^{2}\leq c_{0}^{2}
.
Last, for simplicity, we define
\varepsilon_{h,k}=\frac{r_{h,k}^{2}-\langle\widetilde{{\bm{\phi}}}_{h,k},{\bm{\psi}}_{h}^{*}\rangle}{\sigma_{h,k}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}_{h,k}=\sigma({\mathcal{F}}_{h-1,k}\cup\left\{s_{h,k},a_{h,k}\right\})
.
Then, we have
\varepsilon_{h,k}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{h,k}
,
{\mathbb{E}}[\varepsilon_{h,k}|{\mathcal{G}}_{h,k}]=0
and
\Var[\varepsilon_{h,k}|{\mathcal{G}}_{h,k}]\leq\left(\frac{\sigma_{R^{2}}}{\sigma_{\min}}\right)^{2}:=b^{2}
.
Theorem
3.1
concerns the case where
b=1
, however, its proof considers the general case where
b
can be arbitrary.
As a result, by a similar argument in Appendix
B
(which is doable due to the four conditions mentioned above), once setting
\widetilde{\tau}_{0}\sqrt{\log\frac{2HK^{2}}{\delta}}=\max\left\{\sqrt{2\kappa}b,2\sqrt{d}\right\}
, with probability at least
1-3\delta
, we have for all
h\in[H]
and
k\in[K]
,
\|{\bm{\psi}}_{h,k}-{\bm{\psi}}_{h}^{*}\|_{\widetilde{{\bm{H}}}_{h,k}}\leq\beta_{R^{2}}
, that is the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{R^{2}}
holds.
∎

E.2
Proof of Lemma
C.2

We will make use of the following general result frequently.
The proof is quite standard
[
Jin et al. 2020b
,
Wagenmaker et al. 2022a
,
Hu et al. 2022
]
.
We provide proof in Appendix
E.3
for completeness.

Lemma E.1
.

Fix any
h\in[H]
.
Consider a specific value function
f(\cdot)
which satisfies

(i)

\sup_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}|f(s)|\leq C_{0}
;

(ii)

f\in{\mathcal{V}}
where
{\mathcal{V}}
is a class of functions with
{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{V}},\varepsilon)
the
\varepsilon
-covering number of
{\mathcal{V}}
with respective to the distance
\mathrm{dist}(f,f^{\prime}):=\sup_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}|f(s)-f^{\prime}(s)|
.

We assume there exists a deterministic
C_{\sigma}>0
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{h,k}
(which is
{\mathcal{F}}_{h,k}
-measurable) such that
{\mathcal{A}}_{h,k}\subseteq\left\{\sigma_{h,k}^{2}\geq({\mathbb{V}}_{h}f)(s_{h,k},a_{h,k})/C_{\sigma}^{2}\right\}
for all
k\in[K]
.
Let
{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k}
be defined (
4.2
) and
\sigma_{h,k},{\bm{H}}_{h,k}
be defined in our algorithm.
Under any of the following conditions, with probability at least
1-\delta/H
, it follows for all
k\in[K]\cup\{0\}
,

{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}\in\left\{{\bm{\mu}}:\left\|({\bm{\mu}}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k}){\bm{f}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k}}\leq\beta\right\}.

(E.1)

(i)

If
f(\cdot)
is a deterministic function and
\cap_{k\in[K]}{\mathcal{A}}_{h,k}
is true, (
E.1
) holds with

\beta=8C_{\sigma}\sqrt{d\log\left(1+\frac{K}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}d\lambda}\right)\log\frac{4HK^{2}}{\delta}}+\frac{8C_{0}}{d^{2.5}H}\log\frac{4HK^{2}}{\delta}+\sqrt{d\lambda}C_{0}.

(ii)

If
f(\cdot)
is a random function and
\cap_{k\in[K]}{\mathcal{A}}_{h,k}
is true, (
E.1
) holds with

\beta=8C_{\sigma}\sqrt{d\log\left(1+\frac{K}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}d\lambda}\right)\log\frac{4HK^{2}N_{0}}{\delta}}+\frac{8C_{0}}{d^{2.5}H{\mathcal{H}}}\log\frac{4HK^{2}N_{0}}{\delta}+3\sqrt{d\lambda}C_{0}

where
N_{0}=|{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{V}},\varepsilon_{0})|
and
\varepsilon_{0}=\min\left\{C_{\sigma}\sigma_{\min},\frac{\lambda C_{0}\sqrt{d}}{K}\sigma_{\min}^{2}\right\}
.

(iii)

If
f(\cdot)
is a random function, (
E.1
) holds with

\displaystyle\beta=\frac{2C_{0}}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{d\log\left(1+\frac{K}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}d\lambda}\right)+\log\frac{N_{1}}{\delta}}+3\sqrt{d\lambda}C_{0}.

where
N_{1}=|{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{V}},\varepsilon_{1})|
and
\varepsilon_{1}=\frac{\lambda C_{0}\sqrt{d}}{K}\sigma_{\min}^{2}
.

Using the last item suffices to prove Lemma
C.2
.

Proof of Lemma
C.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{V}}^{+}
denote the class of optimistic value functions mapping from
{\mathcal{S}}
to
{\mathbb{R}}
with the parametric form given in (
F.1
) and
{\mathcal{V}}^{-}
the class of pessimistic value functions with the parametric form given in (
F.2
).
By Lemma
F.8
and Lemma
F.7
,

\displaystyle\log{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{V}}^{\pm},\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq\left[d\log\left(1+\frac{4L}{\varepsilon}\right)+d^{2}\log\left(1+\frac{8d^{1/2}B^{2}}{\lambda\varepsilon^{2}}\right)\right]

(E.2)

where
B\geq\beta_{0}
and
L=W+{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{\frac{dK}{\lambda}}
.

(i)

Let
{\bm{f}}=\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}
.
One can find that
{\bm{f}}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{f}^{+}
with parameter
L=W+\frac{K{\mathcal{H}}}{\lambda\sigma_{\min}^{2}}
.
To plug in Lemma
E.1
, we first specify the parameters defined therein.
We have
\|{\bm{f}}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}={\mathcal{H}}
and
\varepsilon_{1}=\frac{\lambda{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{d}}{K}\sigma_{\min}^{2}
.
By (
E.2
), it follows that

\displaystyle\log{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{V}}^{+},\varepsilon_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\left[d\log\left(1+\frac{4LK}{\lambda{\mathcal{H}}\sqrt{d}\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)+d^{2}\log\left(1+\frac{8B^{2}K^{2}}{\sqrt{d}\lambda^{3}{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\sigma_{\min}^{4}}\right)\right]\cdot dH\log_{2}\left(1+\frac{K}{\lambda\sigma_{\min}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\log 2}d^{3}H\iota_{0}^{2}\leq 3d^{3}H\iota_{0}^{2},

By the third condition of Lemma
E.1
, with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{2H}
,
\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1})\widehat{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\right\|\leq\beta_{0}
for all
k\in[K]
.
Similarly, we can also show that with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{2H}
,
\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1})\widecheck{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\right\|\leq\beta_{0}
for all
k\in[K]
.
Putting them together finishes the proof.

(ii)

The analysis on
\underline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}
is similar to (i).

(iii)

The analysis on
[\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}]^{2}
is similar to (i) except for the following two changes.
First,
C_{0}={\mathcal{H}}^{2}
and
\varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}=\frac{\lambda{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\sqrt{d}}{K}\sigma_{\min}^{2}
.
Second, with
[{\mathcal{V}}^{+}]^{2}=\{f^{2}:f\in{\mathcal{V}}^{+}\}
, we have
[\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}]^{2}\in[{\mathcal{V}}^{+}]^{2}
and

\displaystyle\log{\mathcal{N}}([{\mathcal{V}}^{+}]^{2},\varepsilon_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\log{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{V}}^{+},\frac{\varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}}{2{\mathcal{H}}})\leq\log{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{V}}^{+},\frac{\varepsilon_{1}}{2})\leq 3d^{3}H\iota_{0}^{2}.

Here
(a)
uses the fact that the
\frac{\varepsilon_{1}^{\prime}}{2{\mathcal{H}}}
-cover of
{\mathcal{V}}^{+}
is a
\varepsilon_{1}
-cover of
[{\mathcal{V}}^{+}]^{2}
(which is also supported by Lemma
F.9
).

∎

E.3
Proof of Lemma
E.1

Proof of Lemma
E.1
.

Since the case of
k=0
is trivial, we focus on
k\in[K]
.
By definition,

\displaystyle{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k}

\displaystyle={\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{h,j}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}{\bm{\delta}}(s_{h+1,j})^{\top}={\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{h,j}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}\left({\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}^{\top}{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{h,j}\right)^{\top}

\displaystyle={\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-\lambda{\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{h,j}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{h,j}^{\top}.

By the triangle inequality, it follows that

\displaystyle\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k}){\bm{f}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k}}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\|{\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}{\bm{f}}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k}}+\left\|{\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{h,j}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{h,j}^{\top}{\bm{f}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k}}

\displaystyle=\lambda\|{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}{\bm{f}}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}}+\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{h,j}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{h,j}^{\top}{\bm{f}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{d\lambda}C_{0}+\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{h,j}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{h,j}^{\top}{\bm{f}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}}

where the last inequality uses
\|{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}{\bm{f}}\|\leq\sqrt{d}C_{0}
.

•

Assume
f(\cdot)
is a deterministic function.
To evoke Lemma
F.3
, we set
{\mathcal{G}}_{j}={\mathcal{F}}_{h,j},{\bm{x}}_{j}=\sigma_{h,j}^{-1}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j},\eta_{j}=\sigma_{h,j}^{-1}{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{h,j}^{\top}{\bm{f}}\cdot 1_{{\mathcal{A}}_{h,j}}
and
{\bm{Z}}_{k}=\lambda{\bm{I}}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{h,j}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}^{\top}={\bm{H}}_{h,k}
.
Here
1_{{\mathcal{A}}}
is the indicator function of the event
{\mathcal{A}}
.

Clearly
{\bm{x}}_{j}\in{\mathcal{G}}_{j},{\mathbb{E}}[\eta_{j}|{\mathcal{G}}_{j}]=0
and
{\mathbb{E}}[\eta_{j}^{2}|{\mathcal{G}}_{j}]\leq C_{\sigma}^{2}
.
We also have
\|{\bm{x}}_{j}\|\leq\sigma_{\min}^{-1},|\eta_{j}|\leq 2C_{0}\sigma_{h,j}^{-1}
and
\|{\bm{x}}_{j}\|_{{\bm{Z}}_{j-1}}=w_{h,j}
.
As a result,
|\eta_{j}|\min\left\{1,\|{\bm{x}}_{j}\|_{{\bm{Z}}_{j-1}}\right\}\leq 2C_{0}\frac{w_{h,j}}{\sigma_{h,j}}\leq\frac{2C_{0}}{{\mathcal{H}}d^{2.5}H}
where the last inequality uses
\sigma_{h,j}^{2}\geq{\mathcal{H}}d^{2.5}H\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}
(which is equivalent to
\frac{w_{h,j}}{\sigma_{h,j}}\leq(d^{2.5}H{\mathcal{H}})^{-1}
).
By Lemma Lemma
F.3
, it follows that with probability
1-\frac{\delta}{H}
, for all
k\in[K]
,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{h,j}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{h,j}^{\top}{\bm{f}}1_{{\mathcal{A}}_{h,j}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}}=\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{k}{\bm{x}}_{j}\eta_{j}\right\|_{{\bm{Z}}_{k}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq 8C_{\sigma}\sqrt{d\log\left(1+\frac{K}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}d\lambda}\right)\log\frac{4HK^{2}}{\delta}}+\frac{8C_{0}}{{\mathcal{H}}d^{2.5}H}\log\frac{4HK^{2}}{\delta}.

Finally, on the event
\cap_{k\in[K]}{\mathcal{A}}_{h,k}
, we will have all the indicator functions equal to one.

•

If
f(\cdot)
is a random function, we would use a covering argument to handle the possible correlation between
f(\cdot)
and history data, which would unfortunately enlarge
\beta
.

Denote the
\varepsilon_{0}
-net of
{\mathcal{V}}
by
{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{V}},\varepsilon_{0})
where
\varepsilon_{0}=\min\left\{C_{\sigma}\sigma_{\min},\frac{\lambda C_{0}\sqrt{d}}{K}\sigma_{\min}^{2}\right\}
.
Hence, for any
f\in{\mathcal{V}}
, there exists
\bar{{\bm{f}}}\in{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{V}},\varepsilon_{0})
such that
\|\bar{{\bm{f}}}-{\bm{f}}\|_{\infty}=\sup_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}|f(s)-\bar{f}(s)|\leq\varepsilon_{0}
.
Then,

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{h,j}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{h,j}^{\top}{\bm{f}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{h,j}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{h,j}^{\top}\bar{{\bm{f}}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}}}_{(I)}+\underbrace{\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{h,j}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{h,j}^{\top}({\bm{f}}-\bar{{\bm{f}}})\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}}}_{(II)}.

For the term
(II)
, due to
\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}\|\leq 1
and
|{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{h,j}^{\top}({\bm{f}}-\bar{{\bm{f}}})|\leq\|{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{h,j}\|_{1}\|{\bm{f}}-\bar{{\bm{f}}}\|_{\infty}\leq 2\varepsilon_{0}
, we have

\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{h,j}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{h,j}^{\top}({\bm{f}}-\bar{{\bm{f}}})\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}}\leq\frac{2K\varepsilon_{0}}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}\sqrt{\lambda}}\leq 2\sqrt{d\lambda}C_{0}.

For the term
(I)
, we define
{\mathcal{V}}_{h,k}=\left\{f^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{V}}:4C_{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{h,k}^{2}\geq({\mathbb{V}}_{h}f^{\prime})(s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\right\}
.
Since the definition of
{\mathcal{V}}_{h,k}
involves only
\sigma_{h,k},s_{h,k},a_{h,k}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{h,k}
, for any fixed function
f\in{\mathcal{V}}
,
1_{f\in{\mathcal{V}}_{h,k}}
is
{\mathcal{F}}_{h,k}
-measurable.
On the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{h,k}
, by definition of
\varepsilon_{0}
,

({\mathbb{V}}_{h}\bar{f})(s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\leq 2({\mathbb{V}}_{h}f)(s_{h,k},a_{h,k})+2({\mathbb{V}}_{h}(\bar{f}-f))(s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\leq 2C_{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{h,k}^{2}+2\varepsilon_{0}^{2}\leq 4C_{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{h,k}^{2}.

Hence,
{\mathcal{A}}_{h,k}\subseteq\left\{\sigma_{h,k}^{2}\geq({\mathbb{V}}_{h}f)(s_{h,k},a_{h,k})/C_{\sigma}^{2}\right\}\subseteq\{\exists\bar{f}\in{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{V}},\varepsilon_{0})\cap{\mathcal{V}}_{h,k}\}
for all
k\in[K]
.

In the following, we will evoke Lemma
F.3
to analyze the term
(I)
.
For any fixed
f^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{V}}
, we set
{\mathcal{G}}_{j}={\mathcal{F}}_{h,j},{\bm{x}}_{j}=\sigma_{h,j}^{-1}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j},\eta_{j}=\sigma_{h,j}^{-1}{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{h,j}^{\top}{\bm{f}}^{\prime}\cdot 1_{f^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{h,k}}
and
{\bm{Z}}_{k}=\lambda{\bm{I}}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{h,j}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}^{\top}={\bm{H}}_{h,k}
.
Moreover, due to the choice of
\sigma_{h,j}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\left|\eta_{j}\min\left\{1,\|{\bm{x}}_{j}\|_{{\bm{Z}}_{j-1}^{-1}}\right\}\right|\leq\left|\frac{C_{0}}{\sigma_{h,j}}\right|\cdot\left\|\frac{{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}}{\sigma_{h,j}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,j-1}^{-1}}\leq C_{0}\frac{b_{h,j}}{\sigma_{h,j}^{2}}\leq\frac{C_{0}}{d^{2.5}H{\mathcal{H}}}.

By Lemma
F.3
and the union bound, it follows that with probability
1-\frac{\delta}{H}
, for all
k\in[K]
,

\displaystyle\sup_{f^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{V}},\varepsilon_{0})}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{h,j}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{h,j}^{\top}{\bm{f}}^{\prime}1_{f^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{h,k}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\leq 8C_{\sigma}\sqrt{d\log\left(1+\frac{K}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}d\lambda}\right)\log\frac{4HK^{2}N_{0}}{\delta}}+\frac{8C_{0}}{d^{2.5}H{\mathcal{H}}}\log\frac{4HK^{2}N_{0}}{\delta}.

where
N_{0}=|{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{V}},\varepsilon_{0})|
.

As a result, we know that
\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{h,j}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{h,j}^{\top}\bar{{\bm{f}}}1_{\bar{f}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{h,k}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}}
is no more than the RHS of the last inequality.
On the event
\cap_{k\in[K]}{\mathcal{A}}_{h,k}
, we have
\bar{f}\in\cap_{k\in[K]}{\mathcal{V}}_{h,k}
and thus all the indicator functions equal to one, completing the proof.

•

The proof is almost similar to the second item except that we use Lemma
F.4
to analyze the term
(I)
.
Noticing we also have
|\eta_{j}|=|\sigma_{h,j}^{-1}{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{h,j}^{\top}{\bm{f}}^{\prime}|\leq\frac{2C_{0}}{\sigma_{\min}}
.
By Lemma
F.4
and the union bound, it follows that with probability
1-\frac{\delta}{H}
, for all
k\in[K]
,

\displaystyle\sup_{f^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{V}}_{f},\varepsilon_{1})}\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sigma_{h,j}^{-2}{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}{\bm{\varepsilon}}_{h,j}^{\top}{\bm{f}}^{\prime}1_{f^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{h,k}}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k}^{-1}}\leq\frac{2C_{0}}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{d\log\left(1+\frac{K}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}d\lambda}\right)+\log\frac{N_{1}}{\delta}}.

Pay attention that here we don’t utilize the variance information so that we change
N_{0}:=|{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{V}},\varepsilon_{0})|
to
N_{1}:=|{\mathcal{N}}({\mathcal{V}},\varepsilon_{1})|
and don’t require
\cap_{k\in[K]}{\mathcal{A}}_{h,k}
is true.

∎

E.4
Proof of Lemma
C.3

Proof of Lemma
C.3
.

The proof idea of Lemma
C.3
is similar to that of Lemma
C.1
except that we pay more attention on the reward variance.

Given that
{\mathcal{B}}_{R^{2}}
holds, we have
{\bm{\psi}}_{h}^{*}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{h,k}
for all
h\in[H]
and
k\in[K]\cup\{0\}
.

We will prove the lemma by induction over
k
.
When
k=0
, we have
{\bm{\theta}}_{h,0}=0,{\bm{H}}_{h,0}=\lambda{\bm{I}}
and
\|{\bm{\theta}}_{h,0}-{\bm{\theta}}_{h}^{*}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,0}}=\sqrt{\lambda}\|{\bm{\theta}}_{h}^{*}\|\leq\sqrt{\lambda}W\leq\beta_{R}
for all
h\in[H]
.
If we suppose
{\bm{\theta}}_{h}^{*}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{h,j}
holds for all
h\in[H]
and
j\in[k-1]
, we are going to prove
{\bm{\theta}}_{h}^{*}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{h,k}
uniformly for
h\in[H]
.
The first thing we will show is

\sigma_{h,j}^{2}\geq[\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{h}R_{h}](s_{h,j},a_{h,j})+R_{h,j}\quad\text{for all}\quad h\in[H]\ \text{and}\ j\in[k].

(E.3)

Notice that
[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}R_{h}](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})=\langle\widetilde{{\bm{\phi}}}_{h,k},{\bm{\psi}}_{h}^{*}\rangle-\langle{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k},{{\bm{\theta}}}_{h}^{*}\rangle^{2}
.
We then have for all
h\in[H],j\in[k]
,

\displaystyle|[\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{h}R_{h}-{\mathbb{V}}_{h}R_{h}](s_{h,j},a_{h,j})|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\langle\widetilde{{\bm{\phi}}}_{h,j},{\bm{\psi}}_{h,j-1}\rangle-\langle\widetilde{{\bm{\phi}}}_{h,j},{\bm{\psi}}_{h}^{*}\rangle\right|+\left|\langle{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j},{{\bm{\theta}}}_{h}^{*}\rangle^{2}-\langle{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j},{{\bm{\theta}}}_{h,j-1}\rangle_{[0,{\mathcal{H}}]}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq|\langle\widetilde{{\bm{\phi}}}_{h,k},{\bm{\psi}}_{h,k-1}-{\bm{\psi}}_{h}^{*}\rangle|+2{\mathcal{H}}|\langle{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k},{\bm{\theta}}_{h,j-1}-{\bm{\theta}}_{h}^{*}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\|\widetilde{{\bm{\phi}}}_{h,j}\|_{\widetilde{{\bm{H}}}_{h,j-1}^{-1}}\|{\bm{\psi}}_{h,j-1}-{\bm{\psi}}_{h}^{*}\|_{\widetilde{{\bm{H}}}_{h,j-1}}+2{\mathcal{H}}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,j-1}^{-1}}\|{\bm{\theta}}_{h,j-1}-{\bm{\theta}}_{h}^{*}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,j-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\beta_{R^{2}}\|\widetilde{{\bm{\phi}}}_{h,j}\|_{\widetilde{{\bm{H}}}_{h,j-1}^{-1}}+2{\mathcal{H}}\beta_{R}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,j-1}^{-1}}=R_{h,j}

where the last inequality uses the hypothesis and the condition that
{\mathcal{B}}_{R^{2}}
holds.
As a result, we establish (
E.3
).

Let
{\mathcal{G}}_{h,j}=\sigma({\mathcal{F}}_{h-1,j}\cup\left\{s_{h,j},a_{h,j}\right\})
.
One can show that both
R_{h,j}
and
\sigma_{h,j}^{2}
are
{\mathcal{G}}_{h,j}
-measurable.
As a result, the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{h,j}:=\left\{\sigma_{h,j}^{2}\geq[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}R_{h}](s_{h,j},a_{h,j})\right\}
is also
{\mathcal{G}}_{h,j}
-measurable.
On the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{R^{2}}
, it is obvious that
\cap_{h\in[H]}\cap_{j\in[k]}{\mathcal{E}}_{h,j}
is true since (
E.3
) is true.

On the other hand, we set
\varepsilon_{h,j}=\frac{r_{h,j}-\langle{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j},{\bm{\theta}}_{h}^{*}\rangle}{\sigma_{h,j}}1_{{\mathcal{E}}_{h,j}}
as the standardized reward.
We then have
\varepsilon_{h,j}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{h,j}
,
{\mathbb{E}}[\varepsilon_{h,j}|{\mathcal{G}}_{h,j}]=0
and
\Var[\varepsilon_{h,j}|{\mathcal{G}}_{h,j}]\leq 1
.
We define
\widehat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{h,k}
as the solution of adaptive Huber regression to the response
\{r_{h,j}1_{{\mathcal{E}}_{h,j}}\}_{j\in[k]}
and the feature
\{{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}1_{{\mathcal{E}}_{h,j}}\}_{j\in[k]}
.
We also define
\widehat{{\bm{H}}}_{h,k-1}
as the counterpart matrix of
{\bm{H}}_{h,k}
obtained by replacing
{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}
with
{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}1_{{\mathcal{E}}_{h,k}}
.
We then apply Theorem
3.1
to analyze the concentration of
\widehat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{h,k}
.
With probability at least
1-3\delta
, it follows that
\|\widehat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{h,k}-{\bm{\theta}}_{h}^{*}\|_{\widehat{{\bm{H}}}_{h,k-1}}\leq\beta_{R}
for all
h\in[H]
and
k\in[K]
.
Because
{\mathcal{B}}_{R^{2}}
is true, all indicator functions equal to one.
Therefore, we have
\widehat{{\bm{\theta}}}_{h,k}={\bm{\theta}}_{h,k}
and
\widehat{{\bm{H}}}_{h,k-1}={\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}
, implying
{\bm{\theta}}_{h}^{*}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{h,k}
uniformly for
h\in[H]
.
∎

E.5
Proof of Lemma
C.4

Proof of Lemma
C.4
.

By symmetry, we only prove the RHS inequality, or say, the optimism inequality.
We prove it by induction.
The statement is true for
h=H+1
since both
V_{H+1}^{*}(\cdot)=\overline{V}_{H+1}^{k}(\cdot)=0
for all
k\in[K]
.
Assume the statement is also true for
h+1
, implying
V_{h+1}^{*}(\cdot)\leq\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}(\cdot)
for all
k\in[K]
.
We assume there exists a sequence of updating episodes
1\leq k_{1}<\cdots<k_{N_{k}}\leq K
such that

\overline{Q}_{h}^{k}(\cdot,\cdot)=\min_{i\in[N_{k}]}\left\{\langle{\bm{\phi}}(\cdot,\cdot),{\bm{\theta}}_{h,k_{i}-1}+{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k_{i}-1}\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k_{i}}\rangle+\beta\|{\bm{\phi}}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k_{i}-1}^{-1}},{\mathcal{H}}\right\}.

(E.4)

Using
Q_{h}^{*}(s,a)=\langle{\bm{\phi}}(s,a),{\bm{\theta}}_{h}^{*}+{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}{\bm{V}}_{h+1}^{*}\rangle
, we have for any
(s,a)\in{\mathcal{S}}\times{\mathcal{A}}
and
k\in[K]
,

\displaystyle\langle{\bm{\phi}}(\cdot,\cdot),{\bm{\theta}}_{h,k-1}+{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\rangle+\beta\|{\bm{\phi}}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}-Q_{h}^{*}(\cdot,\cdot)

\displaystyle=\langle{\bm{\phi}}(\cdot,\cdot),{\bm{\theta}}_{h,k-1}-{\bm{\theta}}_{h}^{*}\rangle+\langle{\bm{\phi}}(\cdot,\cdot),{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}{\bm{V}}_{h+1}^{*}\rangle+\beta\|{\bm{\phi}}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\geq}\langle{\bm{\phi}}(\cdot,\cdot),{\bm{\theta}}_{h,k-1}-{\bm{\theta}}_{h}^{*}\rangle+\langle{\bm{\phi}}(\cdot,\cdot),({\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*})\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\rangle+\beta\|{\bm{\phi}}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\geq}\|{\bm{\phi}}(\cdot,\cdot)\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}\left[-\|{\bm{\theta}}_{h,k-1}-{\bm{\theta}}_{h}^{*}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}-\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*})\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}+\beta\right]\overset{(c)}{\geq}0

where
(a)
uses
\langle{\bm{\phi}}(s,a),{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}(\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}-{\bm{V}}_{h+1}^{*})\rangle={\mathbb{P}}_{h}(\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-V_{h+1}^{*})(s,a)\geq 0
from the hypothesis,

(b)
follows from Cauchy-Schwarz inequality
and
(c)
uses
\|{\bm{\theta}}_{h,k-1}-{\bm{\theta}}_{h}^{*}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}+\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*})\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}\leq\beta_{R}+\beta_{V}=\beta
on the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{R}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{h}
.

As a result, by the last inequality and (
E.4
), it follows that for all
k\in[K]
,
\overline{Q}_{h}^{k}(\cdot,\cdot)-Q_{h}^{*}(\cdot,\cdot)\geq 0
. Taking maximum over actions, we have
\overline{V}_{h}^{k}(\cdot)\geq V_{h}^{*}(\cdot)
for all
k\in[K]
, which implies the case of
h
is also true.
∎

E.6
Proof of Lemma
C.5

Proof of Lemma
C.5
.

The proof technique has been used in Lemma C.13 in
[
Hu et al. 2022
]
and Lemma 7.2 in
[
He et al. 2022
]
.
We include the proof for completeness.
By definition,

\displaystyle[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}V_{h+1}^{*}](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})=\langle{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}[{\bm{V}}_{h+1}^{*}]^{2},{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\rangle-\langle{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}{\bm{V}}_{h+1}^{*},{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle[\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{h}\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})=\langle{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}[\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}]^{2},{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k-1}\rangle_{[0,{\mathcal{H}}^{2}]}-\langle{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k},{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\rangle_{[0,{\mathcal{H}}]}^{2}.

Therefore, it follows that

\displaystyle\left|\left[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}V_{h+1}^{*}-\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{h}\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}\right](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{h}\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}\right](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\right|+\left|\left[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}V_{h+1}^{*}-{\mathbb{V}}_{h}\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}\right](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\right|.

We then bound the two terms in the RHS of the last inequality as follows.

\displaystyle\left|\left[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{h}\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}\right](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\langle{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}[\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}]^{2},{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\rangle-\langle{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}[\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}]^{2},{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\rangle_{[0,{\mathcal{H}}^{2}]}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad+\left|\langle{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k},{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\rangle^{2}-\langle{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k},{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\rangle_{[0,{\mathcal{H}}]}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\langle({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1})[\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}]^{2},{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\rangle\right|+2{\mathcal{H}}\cdot\left|\langle{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k},{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\rangle-\langle{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k},{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\rangle_{[0,{\mathcal{H}}]}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*})[\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}]^{2}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}+2{\mathcal{H}}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*})\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}

where the second inequality uses the fact that both
\langle{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k},{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\rangle
and
\langle{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k},{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\rangle_{[0,{\mathcal{H}}]}
lie between
0
and
{\mathcal{H}}
.
Similarly, it follows that

\displaystyle\left|\left[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}V_{h+1}^{*}-{\mathbb{V}}_{h}\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}\right](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|{\mathbb{P}}_{h}[[V_{h+1}^{*}]^{2}-[\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}]^{2}](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\right|+\left|[{\mathbb{P}}_{h}V_{h+1}^{*}]^{2}(s_{h,k},a_{h,k})-[{\mathbb{P}}_{h}\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}]^{2}(s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|{\mathbb{P}}_{h}[(\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-V_{h+1}^{*})(\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}+V_{h+1}^{*})](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\right|

\displaystyle\qquad+\left|[{\mathbb{P}}_{h}\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-{\mathbb{P}}_{h}V_{h+1}^{*}][[{\mathbb{P}}_{h}\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}+{\mathbb{P}}_{h}V_{h+1}^{*}]](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\right|

\displaystyle\leq 4{\mathcal{H}}\cdot{\mathbb{P}}_{h}[\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-V_{h+1}^{*}](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})

\displaystyle\leq 4{\mathcal{H}}\cdot{\mathbb{P}}_{h}[\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-\underline{V}_{h+1}](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})

\displaystyle\leq 4{\mathcal{H}}\cdot\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{h,k}[\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-\underline{V}_{h+1}](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})

\displaystyle\quad+4{\mathcal{H}}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}\cdot\left[\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*})\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}+\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*})\underline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}\right]

where the third and forth inequality we use the optimism and pessimism in Lemma
C.4
and the last inequality uses the following result.

\displaystyle\left|[{\mathbb{P}}_{h}\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{h,k}\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\langle({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1})\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k},{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*})\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}.

A similar inequality can be derived for
\left|[{\mathbb{P}}_{h}\underline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{h,k}\underline{V}_{h+1}^{k}](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\right|
.
Finally, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}V_{h+1}^{*}-\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{h}\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}\right](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*})[\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{*}]^{2}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}+4{\mathcal{H}}\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{h,k}(\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-\underline{V}_{h+1}^{k})(s_{h,k},a_{h,k})

\displaystyle\qquad+{\mathcal{H}}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}^{-1}}\cdot\left[6\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*})\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}+4\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*})\underline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}\right].

We complete the proof by noting that on the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\max\left\{\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1})\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}},\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1})\underline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}\right\}\leq\beta_{0},

\displaystyle\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1})[\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}]^{2}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}\leq{\mathcal{H}}\beta_{0}.

∎

E.7
Proof of Lemma
C.6

Proof of Lemma
C.6
.

For any
j\leq k
, we have

\displaystyle\left[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}(\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-V_{h+1}^{*})\right](s_{h,j},a_{h,j})

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\leq}\left[{\mathbb{P}}_{h}(\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-V_{h+1}^{*})^{2}\right](s_{h,j},a_{h,j})

\displaystyle\overset{(b)}{\leq}{\mathcal{H}}\left[{\mathbb{P}}_{h}(\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-\underline{V}_{h+1}^{k})\right](s_{h,j},a_{h,j})

\displaystyle\overset{(c)}{\leq}{\mathcal{H}}\left[{\mathbb{P}}_{h}(\overline{V}_{h+1}^{j}-\underline{V}_{h+1}^{j})\right](s_{h,j},a_{h,j})

where
(a)
uses the fact that
\Var(X)\leq{\mathbb{E}}X^{2}
for any random variable
X
,
(b)
uses
0\leq\underline{V}_{h+1}^{k}(\cdot)\leq V_{h+1}^{*}(\cdot)\leq\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}(\cdot)\leq{\mathcal{H}}
on the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{R}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{h+1}
from Lemma
C.4
, and
(c)
uses that
\overline{V}_{h+1}^{j}(\cdot)\geq\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}(\cdot)
and
\underline{V}_{h+1}^{j}(\cdot)\leq\underline{V}_{h+1}^{k}(\cdot)
by definition.
On the other hand, on the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\max\left\{\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,j-1})\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{j}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,j-1}},\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,j-1})\underline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{j}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,j-1}}\right\}\leq\beta_{0}.

As a result,

\displaystyle\left[\left({\mathbb{P}}_{h}-\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{h,j}\right)\overline{V}_{h+1}^{j}\right](s_{h,j},a_{h,j})

\displaystyle=\langle{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j},({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,j-1})\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{j}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,j-1}^{-1}}\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,j-1})\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{j}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,j-1}}\leq\beta_{0}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,j-1}^{-1}}.

Similarily, we have
\left[\left({\mathbb{P}}_{h}-\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{h,j}\right)\underline{V}_{h+1}^{j}\right](s_{h,j},a_{h,j})\leq\beta_{0}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,j}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,j-1}^{-1}}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\left[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}(\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}-V_{h+1}^{*})\right](s_{h,j},a_{h,j})

\displaystyle\leq{\mathcal{H}}\left[2\beta_{0}\|{\bm{\phi}}_{h,k}\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,j-1}^{-1}}+\left[\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{h,j}(\overline{V}_{h+1}^{j}-\underline{V}_{h+1}^{j})\right](s_{h,j},a_{h,j})\right]=:E_{h,j}

Repeating the above argument, we have a similar inequality for
\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}
due to symmetry.
∎

E.8
Proof of Lemma
C.7

Proof of Lemma
C.7
.

Due to the backward recursion structure, we will use induction (over horizon
h
) to prove this lemma.
First, (
C.2
) is true for
h=H
since
\overline{V}_{H+1}^{k}(\cdot)=\underline{V}_{H+1}^{k}(\cdot)=0
for all
k\in[K]
.
Therefore, we have
{\mathcal{B}}_{H}
holds.
Assume (
C.2
) holds for horizons no smaller than
h+1
, i.e.,
{\mathcal{B}}_{h+1}
holds with
h+1\leq H
.
In the following, we will show, once
{\mathcal{B}}_{h+1}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
holds,
{\mathcal{B}}_{h}
holds with probability at least than
1-\frac{2\delta}{H}
.
Repeating the argument, we have, given
{\mathcal{B}}_{H}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
holds, with probability at least
1-2\delta
,
{\mathcal{B}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
holds.
Hence,
{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{0}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{1})\geq 1-5\delta
.

Note that

\displaystyle\max

\displaystyle\left\{\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1})\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}},\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1})\underline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}\right\}\leq\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}){\bm{V}}_{h+1}^{*}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}

\displaystyle+\max\left\{\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1})(\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}-{\bm{V}}_{h+1}^{*})\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}},\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1})(\underline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}-{\bm{V}}_{h+1}^{*})\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}\right\}.

we would analyze the two terms in the RHS separately to proceed with the proof.

For the first term

Since
{\bm{V}}_{h+1}^{*}
is a deterministic function, we apply the first item in Lemma
E.1
to bound it.
In the following, we specify the parameters defined therein.
First, we have
C_{0}={\mathcal{H}}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{h,k}=\left\{\sigma_{h,k}^{2}\geq({\mathbb{V}}_{h}V_{h+1}^{*})(s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\right\}
is
{\mathcal{F}}_{h,k}
-measurable.
By Lemma
C.5
, on the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{h+1}
, we have for all
k\in[K]
,
\left|\left[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}V_{h+1}^{*}-\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{h}\overline{V}_{h+1}^{k}\right](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})\right|\leq U_{h,k}
with
U_{h,k}
defined in (
4.9
).
Hence,
\sigma_{h,k}^{2}\geq[\widehat{\mathbb{V}}_{h}\widehat{V}_{h+1}^{k}](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})+U_{h,k}\geq[{\mathbb{V}}_{h}V_{h+1}^{*}](s_{h,k},a_{h,k})
for all
k\in[K]
, implying
\cap_{k\in[K]}{\mathcal{A}}_{h,k}
holds under
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{h+1}
and
C_{\sigma}=1
.
By Lemma
E.1
, with probability at least
1-\frac{\delta}{H}
,
\left\|({\bm{\mu}}_{h}^{*}-{\bm{\mu}}_{h,k-1}){\bm{V}}_{h+1}^{*}\right\|_{{\bm{H}}_{h,k-1}}\leq\beta_{1}
for all
k\in[K]
with
\beta_{1}
defined in the following.
Finally, we simplify
\beta_{1}
as

\displaystyle\beta_{1}

\displaystyle:=8\sqrt{d\log\left(1+\frac{K}{\sigma_{\min}^{2}d\lambda}\right)\log\frac{4HK^{2}}{\delta}}+\frac{8}{d^{2.5}H}\log\frac{4HK^{2}}{\delta}+\sqrt{d\lambda}{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle\leq 8\sqrt{d}\iota_{1}+\frac{8\iota_{1}}{d^{2.5}H}+\sqrt{d\lambda}{\mathcal{H}}\leq 16\sqrt{d}\iota_{1}+\sqrt{d\lambda}{\mathcal{H}}.

For the second term

Since both
\overline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}-{\bm{V}}_{h+1}^{*}
and
\underline{{\bm{V}}}_{h+1}^{k}-{\bm{V}}_{h+1}^{*}
are
{\mathcal{F}}_{H,k-1}
-measurable random functions, we apply the second item in Lemma
E.1
to analyze the second term.
In the following, we specify parameters defined therein.
First,
C_{0}={\mathcal{H}}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{h,k}=\left\{\sigma_{h,k}^{2}\geq d^{3}H\cdot E_{h,k}\right\}
is
{\mathcal{F}}_{h,k}
-measurable.
By Lemma
C.6
, on the event
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{R}\cap{\mathcal{B}}_{h+1}
, we have simultaneously
